<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="References">References</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Diefenbaker&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: References"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Explanatory_notes">Explanatory notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Diefenbaker&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Explanatory notes"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1217336898">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-1"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-1">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Known as the <a href="/wiki/Conservative_Party_of_Canada_(1867%E2%80%931942)" title="Conservative Party of Canada (1867–1942)">Conservatives</a> before 1942.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-anglicized-3"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-anglicized_3-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"> <a href="#CITEREFSmith1995">Smith 1995</a>, p.&#160;3. Following his father's death, William Diefenbaker anglicized the spelling of "Diefenbacher", and changed its pronunciation so that the "baker" part of the name is pronounced like the English word "baker".</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-childhood_ambition-8"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-childhood_ambition_8-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-childhood_ambition_8-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text"> <a href="#CITEREFSmith1995">Smith 1995</a>, p.&#160;14. Note: Upon his brother's accession to the prime ministership, Elmer Diefenbaker sent him a letter recalling this childhood ambition.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-phrasing-9"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-phrasing_9-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"> Note: The exact phrasing of what Diefenbaker said to Laurier varies from source to source.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-H._J._Fraser-35"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-H._J._Fraser_35-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"> <a href="#CITEREFSmith1995">Smith 1995</a>, p.&#160;75. Thirty years later, the winning candidate, H. J. Fraser, challenged Diefenbaker for his parliamentary seat, and was defeated by a 5-to-1&#160;margin. <a href="#CITEREFNewman1963">Newman 1963</a>, p.&#160;21.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-1957_seats-83"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-1957_seats_83-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"> <a href="#CITEREFMeisel1962">Meisel 1962</a>, p.&#160;291. The 112th seat was not obtained until July 15, as the election in one riding was not held until then due to the death of the original Liberal candidate. <a href="#CITEREFMeisel1962">Meisel 1962</a>, p.&#160;235. Additionally, the Liberal victory in Yukon was vacated by the <a href="/wiki/Yukon_Territorial_Court" class="mw-redirect" title="Yukon Territorial Court">Yukon Territorial Court</a> and a Tory won the new election in December 1957. <a href="#CITEREFMeisel1962">Meisel 1962</a>, p.&#160;239.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-Avro_jobs-143"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Avro_jobs_143-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"> <a href="#CITEREFSmith1995">Smith 1995</a>, pp.&#160;317–320. Over 50,000 other jobs were affected in the supply chain. <a href="#CITEREFPeden1987">Peden 1987</a>, p.&#160;157.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-Kim_Campbell-210"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Kim_Campbell_210-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"> <a href="/wiki/Kim_Campbell" title="Kim Campbell">Kim Campbell</a> also became a PC Prime Minister, but she never won an election to gain that role.</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Further_reading">Further reading</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Diefenbaker&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Further reading"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li>Bothwell, Robert, Ian Drummond, and John English. "The Diefenbaker Years 1957-63." in <i>Canada Since 1945</i> (University of Toronto Press, 2018). pp 181–252; university textbook</li>
<li>Boyko, John. <i>Cold fire: Kennedy's northern front</i> (Alfred A. Knopf Canada, 2016)</li>
<li>Carter, Mark. "Diefenbaker's Bill of Rights and the Counter-Majoritarian Difficulty: The Notwithstanding Clause and Fundamental Justice as Touchstones for the Charter Debate." <i>Saskatchewan Law Review</i> 82 (2019): 121+ <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.canlii.org/w/canlii/2019CanLIIDocs2717.pdf">online</a>.</li>
<li>Cavell, Janice, and Ryan M. Touhey, eds. <i>Reassessing the Rogue Tory: Canadian Foreign Relations in the Diefenbaker Era</i> (UBC Press, 2018).</li>
<li>Empey, Sarah. "John G. Diefenbaker and Cross Border Relations During the Bomarc Missile Crisis." <i>Waterloo Historical Review</i> 8 (2016). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://openjournals.uwaterloo.ca/index.php/whr/article/download/68/60">online</a></li>
<li>Hilliker, John. "The Politicians and the 'Pearsonalities': The Diefenbaker Government and the Conduct of Canadian External Relations", in <i>Canadian Foreign Policy: Historical Readings</i> ed. J. L. Granatstein (Toronto: Copp Clark Pitman, 1993), pp 152–167.</li>
<li>Kyba, Patrick. <i>Alvin: A Biography of the Honourable Alvin Hamilton, PC</i> (Regina: Canadian Plains Research Center, 1989).</li>
<li>McKercher, Asa. "No, Prime Minister: Revisiting Diefenbaker and the 'Pearsonalities'." <i>Canadian Journal of History</i> 52.2 (2017): 264–289. <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.academia.edu/download/58382440/Pearsonalities.pdf">online</a><sup class="noprint Inline-Template"><span style="white-space: nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Link_rot" title="Wikipedia:Link rot"><span title="&#160;Dead link tagged July 2022">dead link</span></a></i>&#93;</span></sup></li>
<li>McKercher, Asa. "Sound and Fury: Diefenbaker, Human Rights, and Canadian Foreign Policy." <i>Canadian Historical Review</i> 97.2 (2016): 165–194. <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.academia.edu/download/58382438/Sound_and_Fury.pdf">online</a><sup class="noprint Inline-Template"><span style="white-space: nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Link_rot" title="Wikipedia:Link rot"><span title="&#160;Dead link tagged July 2022">dead link</span></a></i>&#93;</span></sup></li>
<li>McKercher, Asa. "The trouble with self-determination: Canada, Soviet colonialism and the United Nations, 1960–1963." <i>The International Journal of Human Rights</i> 20.3 (2016): 343–364.</li>
<li>McMahon. Patricia I. <i>Essence of Indecision: Diefenbaker's Nuclear Policy, 1957–1963</i> (McGill-Queen's University Press, 2009) <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.h-net.org/reviews/showrev.php?id=36675">online review</a></li>
<li>Molinaro, Dennis. "'Calculated Diplomacy': John Diefenbaker and the Origins of Canada's Cuba Policy." in <i>Our place in the sun</i> (University of Toronto Press, 2016) pp.&#160;75–95.</li>
<li>Manulak, Daniel. "Blood Brothers: Moral Emotion, the Afro-Asian-Canadian Bloc, and South Africa's Expulsion from the Commonwealth, 1960–1." <i>Canadian Historical Review</i> (2021): e20200041.</li>
<li>Morris-Hurl, Rebecca. "Diefenbaker's Canada: A Vision for Human Rights and Multiculturalism in the Speeches from the Throne." in <i>Canada and Speeches from the Throne</i> (2020). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://opentextbooks.uregina.ca/primeministers2020/chapter/johndiefenbaker/">online</a></li>
<li>Neary, Peter. "High Commissioner JJS Garner on Joey Smallwood versus John Diefenbaker, 1959." <i>Newfoundland and Labrador Studies</i> 32.1 (2017): 229–240. <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.erudit.org/en/journals/nflds/1990-v6-n1-nflds32_1/nflds32_1doc02/">online</a></li>
<li>Parker, Oliver. "Canadian Concerns of a Different Kind of Brexit: Britain's First Application to the EEC and Canada's Commonwealth Appeal." <i>The Round Table</i> 108.1 (2019): 81–85.</li>
<li>Story, D. C. and R. Bruce Shepard, eds. <i>Diefenbaker legacy: Canadian politics, law and society since 1957</i>. (Canadian Plains Research Center, University of Regina, 1998). 13 essays by experts.</li>
<li>Stevenson, Michael D. "George Drew, the Law of the Sea, and the Diefenbaker Government, 1957-1963." <i>Diplomacy &amp; Statecraft</i> 31.2 (2020): 326–349.</li>
<li>Urban, Michael Crawford. "A fearful asymmetry: Diefenbaker, the Canadian military and trust during the Cuban missile crisis." <i>Canadian Foreign Policy Journal</i> 21.3 (2015): 257–271. <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.academia.edu/download/43425858/Urban_2015_A_fearful_asymmetry_-_Prize.pdf">online</a><sup class="noprint Inline-Template"><span style="white-space: nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Link_rot" title="Wikipedia:Link rot"><span title="&#160;Dead link tagged July 2022">dead link</span></a></i>&#93;</span></sup></li>
